- **sensei-master-copy**: Especialista em copywriting persuasivo, profissional e organizado para comunicação de dados e informações complexas. Use para: criar textos persuasivos, estruturar argumentos complexos, apresentar dados de forma clara e envolvente, e aplicar técnicas dos maiores mestres do copywriting mundial. ([taleshagn-horushub/thoth](https://github.com/taleshagn-horushub/thoth/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-master-copy) · ★1)
- **sensei-prove-it**: Evaluate what the tests actually prove and what they miss. Use when reviewing a PR's test coverage, when a developer says "I wrote tests", or when you want to challenge the developer to reason about test quality — not just test quantity. ([onehorizonai/sensei](https://github.com/onehorizonai/sensei/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-prove-it) · ★3)
- **sensei-prune**: Guide a developer through safe, incremental, behavior-preserving refactoring. Use when a developer says "I want to refactor this", "how do I clean this up safely", or "there's tech debt here". If the request changes behavior, route to gameplan or trace first. Do not write the refactored code; help the developer choose the next verified move. ([onehorizonai/sensei](https://github.com/onehorizonai/sensei/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-prune) · ★3)
- **sensei-reflect**: Run a post-merge or post-session reflection to capture what was learned and identify what to practice next. Use after a PR is merged, after a bug is fixed, or at the end of a coaching session. Keep it short enough to review in two minutes. ([onehorizonai/sensei](https://github.com/onehorizonai/sensei/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-reflect) · ★3)
- **sensei-scroll**: Help a developer write a clear PR description that communicates intent, risks, verification, and reviewer focus. Use before a PR is opened or when the PR description is thin or missing. Reads the diff first, pre-fills what can be observed, then challenges the developer to own the WHY, risks, and verification. ([onehorizonai/sensei](https://github.com/onehorizonai/sensei/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-scroll) · ★3)
- **sensei-smell**: Analyze code for responsibility, boundary, abstraction, code-bloat, and duplicated-knowledge problems. Use when a function or module seems to be doing too many things, when the same business rule appears in multiple places, when the developer asks about SRP, DRY, KISS, code smells, or whether code is too complex, or when reviewing whether a split or abstraction is justified. ([onehorizonai/sensei](https://github.com/onehorizonai/sensei/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-smell) · ★3)
- **sensei-spar**: Review a code diff or file for correctness, security risks, AI slop, code smells, pattern mismatches, and missing verification — in teaching mode. Use when a developer asks for a code review, "look at this diff", "review my PR", or wants to know whether code is simple, maintainable, or too hacky. ([onehorizonai/sensei](https://github.com/onehorizonai/sensei/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-spar) · ★3)
- **sensei-trace**: Guide a developer through debugging without jumping to a fix. Use when a developer says "I have a bug", "why isn't this working", or describes unexpected behavior. Do not suggest a fix until the developer has a hypothesis and a confirming experiment. The goal is to teach the debugging process, not to find the bug. ([onehorizonai/sensei](https://github.com/onehorizonai/sensei/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-trace) · ★3)
- **sensei-tradeoff**: Help a developer reason through a design decision by naming options, costs, constraints, reversibility, and what would change the decision. Use when a developer says "should I use X or Y", "help me decide", "what's the tradeoff", or "is this the right architecture". If the decision claims architecture fit, read the closest local precedent before judging. Do not decide for the developer. ([onehorizonai/sensei](https://github.com/onehorizonai/sensei/tree/HEAD/skills/sensei-tradeoff) · ★3)
- **sensei-tutor**: Use whenever acting as Sensei the tutor for the learner (in Slack or chat) — answering about today's topic, lessons, progress, or resources; marking lessons/quizzes complete; or changing the plan. Guarantees friendly, non-technical replies over the Supabase curriculum; never exposes SQL, tables, or IDs. ([koshikraj/sensei](https://github.com/koshikraj/sensei/tree/HEAD/plugins/sensei/skills/sensei-tutor) · ★1)
- **Sensei:Report**: Session report generation and knowledge gap management for Sensei Mode. ([mec07/claude_skills](https://github.com/mec07/claude_skills/tree/HEAD/stow/Sensei/Report) · ★1)
- **Sensei:Scaffolding**: 5-level adaptive scaffolding rules and Socratic question taxonomy for Sensei Mode. ([mec07/claude_skills](https://github.com/mec07/claude_skills/tree/HEAD/stow/Sensei/Scaffolding) · ★1)
